The limiting multipliers are $\Theta(0,\pi^2/(3\Lambda^3))=1.759797211458$ and
$\Theta(\frac12,\cdot)=0.309288136393$ at $\Lambda=4$, and $2.459097973253$ and
$0.042574972058$ at $\Lambda=5$.  The cases approach these strongly non-unit
values.  At $\alpha=8$, and less so at $\alpha=12$, rare omissions of low parts
have not yet become negligible in the count $R_{\ge2}$, which explains the
appreciable mismatch; the proof asserts no effective onset.  Including part
one in the count would predict values close to $1$: at $\alpha=32$ the wrong
phase-$0$ and half-phase values are about $0.9948$ and $1.0052$ for $\Lambda=4$,
and $0.9914$ and $1.0086$ for $\Lambda=5$.

\paragraph{Method and accuracy limits \src{100-93}.}
The program keeps the frozen baselines $\lfloor M\rfloor-1$ (for $R_{\ge2}$) and
$\lfloor M\rfloor(\lfloor M\rfloor+1)/2-1$ (for $S$) as exact integers: below the
upper edge deviations are rare holes, above it rare additions; means are
accumulated as corrections, $v_k$ is formed from the rare probability, and
$Q_\perp$ is evaluated through \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:Qexact}, avoiding the
subtraction of nearly equal variances; part one is kept separately in $S$.
The centred characteristic function is integrated around $\theta_j$ in the
coordinate $y=\sqrt B(\theta-\theta_j)$, with 64-bit-mantissa extended precision,
160-point Gauss--Legendre quadrature on $[-11,11]$, degree-20 or degree-30
discrete-cumulant acceleration with exact rational Bernoulli cumulant
coefficients, and an independent check of the full rare-variable product at
five points per satellite; satellites $|j|\le8$ are kept for $\Lambda=4$ and
$|j|\le10$ for $\Lambda=5$.  Omitted arcs are assessed by pointwise algebraic
envelopes built from consecutive edge blocks, not by the limiting theta tail;
their integrated normalized estimates are below $3.4\cdot10^{-14}$, but the
integration uses ordinary adaptive quadrature, not interval arithmetic.  The
discarded-variable coupling bounds are below $6.4\cdot10^{-22}$; cutoff,
window, node-count, Taylor-degree and satellite-count changes support about
12 significant digits.  An independent C++ binary128 computation evaluates
the full product at every node without acceleration: for $\alpha=8$, target
$\Lambda=4$, phase $0$, $n=3320242$ its retained-arc multiplier is
$1.742802903877193692$ (96 and 128 nodes agree through 33 digits; the
accelerated value differs by $6.2\cdot10^{-15}$), and for $\alpha=12$, target
$\Lambda=5$, half phase, $n=21224105$, it is $0.042272999079952589$ (31 digits;
difference $1.6\cdot10^{-16}$).  These digits concern retained arcs only.
\mergenoted{The intake reran source 100-93's exact identity checks (eight
algebraic identities, among them \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:Qexact},
\eqref{dpn:ct:eq:betadiff}, \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:variancecomplete} and
\eqref{dpn:ct:eq:Qt}) and its consistency checks of the 18 saved rows on
copies, and compiled and ran the binary128 program, reproducing all four of
its outputs; the main extended-precision runs were not repeated.}

\subsection{Further questions and research (source 100-93)}\label{dpn:ct:sec:questions}
Source 100-93's six questions are kept with its numbers; the seventh is
added by this write.  No claim of source 100-93 was found to be wrong; every
step of its main chain was checked at placement.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{100-93.\arabic*}, leftmargin=*]
\item \textbf{Effective errors.}  Explicit constants for the edge
  approximation, the satellite remainders and the integrated complement, to
  make finite threshold brackets certifiable (overlaps project~8 and 100.2).
\item \textbf{Higher satellite corrections.}  Uniform cumulant corrections,
  summed as theta derivatives; the cubic bound \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:cubic} need not
  give the optimal rate.  (Part~II of the lattice-peak report has a
  theta-derivative calculus for its all-orders transfer theorem.)
\item \textbf{Sharper inversion.}  Enough uniform correction orders for a
  logarithmic forward error $o(t)$ and an inverse error $o(1)$; exact-range
  rounding would then need its own error-below-one-half argument.
\item \textbf{Small critical parameter.}  Uniformity as $\Lambda\downarrow0$,
  where the first Fourier correction has size $\exp[-2\pi^4/(3\Lambda^3)]$,
  matching the single-Gaussian regime; at fixed $\alpha$ this is the scale of
  Theorem~\ref{dpn:thm:resonance} (overlaps project~4).
\item \textbf{Large critical parameter.}  $\Lambda\to\infty$, where the
  conditional variance decreases and the multiplier concentrates on the count
  phase; the lower bound \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:thetalower} is not uniform there.  The
  diagonal \OEIS{A292190} ($\alpha=n$) has $\Lambda\to\infty$ and lies
  here (overlaps project~4, 75-01.4, 75-06.4).
\item \textbf{Other inside weights.}  $w_k=\exp(\alpha\log k+g(k))$ with a
  comparable upper-edge width: which lower parts stay non-frozen, and how their
  finite factors change the theta law (overlaps project~7).
\item \textbf{Fibre lattice peaks} (\wrt).  Prove (H1) and (H2e) of the
  lattice-peak transfer theorem for the row $l\mapsto\Prob_t(S=n,R_{\ge2}=l)$ in
  the critical window: a local limit theorem for $(S,R_{\ge2})$ on the fibre
  $S=n$, uniform for $|l-\mu_R|\le C$, and a summable majorant, for instance
  from log-concavity in $l$.  This would make Theorem~\ref{dpn:ct:thm:theta} an
  instance of Theorem \texttt{tbs:lpt:thm:transfer}.  Sketch: the
  two-dimensional Fourier integral over $(\theta,\omega)\in[-\pi,\pi]^2$ with the
  methods of Lemmas~\ref{dpn:ct:lem:local}--\ref{dpn:ct:lem:integrated};
  missing: the two-dimensional complement bound, and log-concavity.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent Source 100-89's question of a uniform theory for growing $\alpha$
(question~100.7) is answered in the window by this section.

\paragraph{Independent check of the write (dated note, 5~October 2026).}
An independent adversarial check of the five results this write supplied in
Sections~\ref{dpn:rb:sec:b100} and~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:theta}, made by the intake
after the write, found Lemma~\ref{dpn:rb:lem:holo},
Proposition~\ref{dpn:rb:prop:slope}, Remark~\ref{dpn:rb:rem:sharp} and the
phase average \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:average} valid, and
Remark~\ref{dpn:ct:rem:shift} valid with one overstatement (``of exact order
$1/t$'', false at the two zeros of $\log\Theta$ per period); that wording and
an understated displacement term in Remark~\ref{dpn:rb:rem:sharp} are
corrected where they stand, with dated notes.  It found no counterexample and
no gap in any proof chain.  It reread every step and tested them numerically
against exact coefficients of $\prod_k(1+k^\alpha q^k)$, computed by a
big-integer recursion to $n=4000$ for $\alpha=1,2,3$ (the first two are
\OEIS{A022629} and \OEIS{A092484}) and by tilted binary64 recursions for
$\alpha=1.5,6,8,10$.  For Lemma~\ref{dpn:rb:lem:holo} it evaluated the
boundary functional at complex $\alpha$ by Euler--Maclaurin: it reproduces
the printed $\xi_0(1),\dots,\xi_3(1)$, $\xi_0(2)$, $\xi_1(2)$ to 19--25 digits,
satisfies the Cauchy--Riemann equations to $10^{-11}$ or better at four points
(two with $\Re\alpha<1$), and its circle means about $\alpha=1,2$ reproduce the
real constants to $4\cdot10^{-10}$.  For
Proposition~\ref{dpn:rb:prop:slope} the numerical derivative of the inverse
model matches \eqref{dpn:rb:eq:Psiprime} to $5\cdot10^{-11}$ or better ($\alpha=1,1.5,2,3$,
$n=250$--$4000$, $J=1,2,3$), and both error terms stay bounded on their
scales.  For Remark~\ref{dpn:rb:rem:sharp} the lattice--integral replacement
alone is a stable multiple of $t^{D+1}$ ($\alpha=0.5,2,3$, $n\le8000$), and at
$\alpha=1$ the full residual over $\epsh^J$ stays bounded, tending to $-3/8$
for $J=1$ (the sharp $\mathcal E_1$); at $\alpha=2,3$ and $n\le4000$ the
residual is dominated by the noncentral Fourier cells of
Section~\ref{dpn:jh:sec:numerics}, exponentially small only asymptotically.
For \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:average} both forms of \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:Theta}
integrate to $1$ to 16 digits for $0.02\le Q\le3$.  For
Remark~\ref{dpn:ct:rem:shift}, at exact lattice saddles ($\alpha=8$--$32$,
$\Lambda=4,5$, about three phase periods)
$|(n_\Theta-n_*)t+\log\Theta|\le6\cdot10^{-6}$ while $(n_\Theta-n_*)t$ ranges
over $[-0.88,3.09]$ with both signs; at exact thresholds ($\alpha=6,8,10$,
$n\approx2$--$3\cdot10^4$) the displacement changes sign with the phase and
correlates with $-\log\Theta/t$ at $0.90$--$0.99$, with magnitudes still
preasymptotic; this is an illustration only.  This was a careful reading with numerical tests, not a
formal verification or an external review.  A numerical-method note: the
closed form \eqref{dpn:rb:eq:xir}, evaluated with \texttt{mpmath.nsum} at
$(r,\alpha)=(2,4.5)$ and $(3,5.5)$, differs from the Euler--Maclaurin functional
by about $2\cdot10^{-7}$, the accuracy of \texttt{nsum} on these slowly
convergent sums (compare the merge note after \eqref{dpn:rb:eq:xi0}); it is
not a defect of the formula.

\section{Compact proposed OEIS formula updates}
For the entry corresponding to $\alpha=1,\ldots,5$, write
$K=\sqrt{2n}$ and $r=\log K$. The following common formula is proved in
Theorem~\ref{dpn:thm:log}:
\[
 \log a_\alpha(n)=\alpha K(r-1)
       +\frac{\pi^2K}{6\alpha r}
       +\frac{\pi^2K}{6\alpha r^2}
       +O_\alpha(K/r^3).
\]
It is appropriate to link a reviewed proof before changing a database's
conjectural status. This report has not submitted or edited any OEIS entry.
For the array \OEIS{A292189}, the result holds down every fixed positive
column; it makes no uniform assertion about the main diagonal or columns
whose index grows with the row.
\writenoted{Columns whose index grows like $\alpha\asymp(2n)^{1/6}/\log n$
are now covered, with a theta-corrected equivalent and the leading logarithm
$\alpha K(r-1)$ (Section~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:theta}); the main diagonal is not.}

\subsection*{Source 48's suggested A022629 addition}
Source 48 suggests the following text for independent editorial review; it
has not been submitted.
\begin{quote}
The logarithmic conjecture follows with the stronger estimate
$\log a(n)=\sqrt{2n}(\log\sqrt{2n}-1)+O(\sqrt n/\log n)$.
More precisely, with $K=\sqrt{2n}$ and $r=\log K$,
\[
 \log a(n)=K\left(r-1+\frac{\pi^2}{6r}
  +\frac{\pi^2}{6r^2}
  +\frac{\pi^2/6-\pi^4/72}{r^3}
  +O(r^{-4})\right).
\]
A proof, an all-orders coefficient rule, and a separate relative saddle-point
formula are supplied in the accompanying research report.
\end{quote}
A particularly short proof of the first assertion uses the partition
$(1,2,\ldots,m-1,m+d)$ with $n=m(m+1)/2+d$ for the lower bound, and
$a(n)\le e^{tn}\prod_k(1+ke^{-tk})$ with $t=\log\sqrt{2n}/\sqrt{2n}$ for the
upper bound.  Splitting the logarithm into its positive-part contribution and
a smoothing remainder of width $O(\sqrt n/\log n)$ finishes the argument; see
Section~\ref{dpn:48:sec:elementary}.
\mergenote{The ``accompanying research report'' is now this report.}

\subsection*{Sources 75-01 and 75-06's suggested notes}
Both sources ship draft OEIS notes, not submitted or approved:
\file{75-01-jensen-OEIS_PROPOSED_NOTES.md} and
\file{75-06-powers-proposed_oeis_updates.txt}.  Beyond the expansion above
(source 75-01 through $r^{-5}$, source 75-06 through $r^{-4}$, both in the
form $\pi^{2j}$ or $u^j$), source 75-01 proposes for A022629 the log-concavity
gap $\log(a(n)^2/(a(n-1)a(n+1)))\sim\log\sqrt{2n}/(2n)^{3/2}$, eventual
hyperbolicity of fixed-degree Jensen polynomials (by the
Griffin--Ono--Rolen--Zagier mechanism, which is not new), and the finite
observation that $n=77,\dots,9999$ are strictly log-concave, explicitly not a
proof that $77$ is the threshold.  Source 75-06 proposes the same formula for
the positive columns of A292189 and, with $\mu K_\mu$ for $\alpha K$ and
$u=\pi^2$, for the positive columns of A297321.  Both insist that a fixed
truncation of a logarithmic expansion is not a relative asymptotic, and both
ask for review and a broader priority search before use.

100-89 & \file{ProveIt_Power_Weighted_Distinct_Partitions_Relative_Expansions.zip}
  (\texttt{60f54ea06}) & \file{article.tex}, 9 pp. & none & the same\\
100-93 & \file{ProveIt_Critical_Theta_Power_Weighted_Distinct_Partitions.zip}
  (\texttt{60f54ea06}) & \file{article.tex} and three included files, 13 pp. &
  \texttt{6591a56b3} & the same\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Both are manuscripts of batch 73 (cluster O1) of ProveIt's incoming-reports
intake, numbered 40 and 48 there; the placement commit \texttt{9df4ba51a}
staged their files and deleted the archives, which survive in their arrival
commits.  Source 40 arrived first.  Neither cites the other, and their texts
share almost nothing (8-word shingle containment $0.004$ both ways; the
longest common run is a \verb|pdflatex| command line): they are independent.
Both pins are ProveIt commits of 1 October 2026; source 48 calls its
identifier a ``tree response'' and does not assert that it pins every
subsequent read.

\emph{What each contributed.}  Source 40: the base text, in its order, and
everything in it, including the five sequences, $P_7$, $P_8$, the resonance
theorem and the finite-size analysis.  Source 48: the second proofs of
Proposition~\ref{dpn:prop:elementary}, Lemmas~\ref{dpn:lem:scales}
and~\ref{dpn:lem:block}, Theorem~\ref{dpn:thm:saddle} and the threshold
transfer; Propositions~\ref{dpn:48:prop:zeros}
and~\ref{dpn:48:prop:maxweight}; Lemma~\ref{dpn:48:lem:width}; the operator
route; Corollaries~\ref{dpn:48:cor:entropy} and~\ref{dpn:48:thm:gumbel}; the
A022629 forms; its computations, projects, OEIS wording and audit table.

\emph{Where the merge chose.}
\begin{itemize}
\item Base: source 40, the more general manuscript (five sequences, one more
      coefficient, the resonance theorem).
\item Notation: source 40's throughout; source 48's symbols renamed as in
      Section~\ref{dpn:sec:notation}.
\item Source 48's $\lambda$-centred Gumbel law is printed as a corollary of
      Theorem~\ref{dpn:thm:geometry} with its own proof, since its centring
      is explicit where source 40's is the exact root $b(t_n)$.
\item Source 48's statements that duplicate source 40's (its Theorem~1.1,
      Theorem~2.4, Lemma~3.1, Lemma~3.2, Theorem~3.3, Theorem~6.1,
      Proposition~7.2) are printed once, credited, with only their different
      proofs or forms kept.  Its abstract, title page and package-contents
      paragraph are replaced by this text and the README.
\item Both data tables of A022629 are shipped (they agree on $n\le5000$).
\item The two bibliographies are merged; source 48's OEIS item is source 40's
      \cite{oeis1}.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Batch 75.}
Sources 75-01 and 75-06 are manuscripts 01 and 06 of batch 75 of the intake,
both in the arrival commit \texttt{4b874cea0}; the placement commit
\texttt{6ea60e367} staged their files under the prefixes \file{75-01-jensen-}
and \file{75-06-powers-} and deleted the archives, which survive in the
arrival commit.  The prefixes continue this report's source-numbered scheme,
with the batch named so that \file{01-} and \file{06-} cannot be read as
batch-73 manuscript numbers.  Both pin
\texttt{29aca108ed25d714f31b1316512777fbbdc8e006} (1 October 2026), which
already held the archives of sources 40 and 48 unopened.  Their texts share
$0.55$--$1.41\,\%$ of their word 8-grams with sources 40 and 48 and with each
other (preamble, OEIS references and standard displays): they are independent.

\emph{What each contributed.}  Source 75-01: Lemma~\ref{dpn:jh:lem:cumulants}
and Remark~\ref{dpn:jh:rem:naive}, Theorems~\ref{dpn:jh:thm:saddle},
\ref{dpn:jh:thm:threshold}, \ref{dpn:jh:thm:concavity} and~\ref{dpn:jh:thm:jensen},
the smooth approximant of Section~\ref{dpn:jh:sec:shape}, the finite data of
Tables~\ref{dpn:jh:tab:errors} and~\ref{dpn:jh:tab:roots}, the analytic-inverse
route of Section~\ref{dpn:sec:b75routes}, ten research questions and its OEIS
notes.  Source 75-06: the second proof of
Lemma~\ref{dpn:jh:lem:cumulants}, Section~\ref{dpn:cp:sec:powers} (the
$\mu$-layer theorems, $\Delta_7$, $\Delta_8$, the OEIS identifications), the
exact $b_8$, the coefficient-structure remark, the finite-size readings,
twelve research questions and its OEIS notes.

\emph{Where the batch-75 merge chose.}
\begin{itemize}
\item Placement: both sources were added to this report rather than filed
      separately; a separate report would have repeated Sections~2--10.  The
      convolution powers were not split off either: their coefficients are
      the same $P_j$, and every proof is this report's at $n/\mu$.
\item Duplicates: every result already proved here is recorded in
      Table~\ref{dpn:tab:b75dup} and not reprinted; second-route text is kept
      only where the argument differs (Section~\ref{dpn:sec:b75routes}, the
      second proof of Lemma~\ref{dpn:jh:lem:cumulants}, and the $\mu$-layer
      modifications of Section~\ref{dpn:cp:sec:powers}).
\item Placement in the text: the new sections follow the conclusion, so that
      every earlier section, theorem, equation and table keeps its number;
      unnumbered merge notes in the earlier sections point forward.
\item Base for the shared new results: source 75-01 for the sharp cumulant
      scales and the remainder theorem (it proves the full remainder),
      source 75-06's Proposition~5.4 as the second proof.
\item Notation: this report's throughout; both sources' symbols are renamed as
      in the second table of Section~\ref{dpn:sec:notation}.  New symbols:
      $\mu$, $K_\mu$, $r_\mu$, $\epsh$, $n_*$, $\Delta_j$, $\mathcal J$,
      $\mathsf H_d$, $\tau_n$, $\gamma_k$, $\mathcal R(n)$.
\item Source 75-01's finite-size residuals at $\alpha=2,3$, which it reads as
      slow convergence, are kept with its own words and identified by a merge
      note as the noncentral contribution of Section~\ref{dpn:sec:resonance}.
\item Both sources' novelty paragraphs and abstracts are replaced by
      Section~\ref{dpn:sec:b75}; their priority caveats are kept there.
\item Source 75-01's tables for $\alpha=2,3$ duplicate source 40's and are not
      shipped; its $\alpha=1$ table, which extends the range to $n=10000$, is.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Batch 77.}
Source 77-51 is manuscript 51 of batch 77 of the intake (cluster P2), in the
arrival commit \texttt{096ee7b87}; the placement commit \texttt{aa7345800}
staged its 33 support files under the prefix \file{77-51-fp-} and deleted the
archive, which survives in the arrival commit.  The prefix continues this
report's source-numbered scheme, with the batch named as for batch 75.  The
archive has no wrapper directory; its three
\file{*-optimization-negative-control.txt} files were shortened to
\file{*-negative-control.txt} to keep paths short.  Source 77-51 names no
ProveIt commit (its approval records name only the producer's working
directory) and records no repository search.  Its text shares $0.69\,\%$ of
its word 8-grams with this report and $0.11\,\%$ in the other direction: it is
independent.  Its checksum ledger \file{SHA256SUMS} was verified (36/36) and
retired.

\emph{What it contributed.}  The fifth proofs recorded in
Table~\ref{dpn:fp:tab:dup}; Proposition~\ref{dpn:fp:prop:thermal} (the
resummed forms); the cutoff-sum route, the coefficient generation in its
variables and the exact-saddle inverse of Section~\ref{dpn:fp:sec:routes};
Theorem~\ref{dpn:fp:thm:family} with its monotonicity, uniform estimates and
constants; Corollary~\ref{dpn:fp:cor:a266891}; the citation of Naranjo and
Ram\'irez; Table~\ref{dpn:fp:tab:numerics}; four research questions.

\emph{Where the batch-77 merge chose.}
\begin{itemize}
\item Placement: added to this report, not filed separately, because its
      core theorems are this report's and its family contains this report's
      at $\beta=0$.
\item Duplicates: recorded in Table~\ref{dpn:fp:tab:dup} and not reprinted;
      the resummed forms, the cutoff-sum route, the generation in its
      variables and the exact-saddle inverse are kept because they differ in
      form or route.
\item Placement in the text: Section~\ref{dpn:fp:sec:b77} follows the
      batch-75 sections and precedes the appendices, so every earlier
      number is unchanged.
\item Notation: this report's throughout, with the family notation
      $A^{[\beta]}_\alpha$ new; source 77-51's symbols are renamed as in the
      third table of Section~\ref{dpn:sec:notation}.
\item Bibliography: source 77-51's OEIS items for A022629 and A092484 are
      \cite{oeis1} and \cite{oeis2}, its Granovsky--Stark item is \cite{GS},
      and its Gikunda item is \cite{gikunda} (with the DOI rather than either
      of its two ResearchGate addresses); \cite{NR} and \cite{oeis266891} are
      new.
\item Its abstract, title, novelty sentences and package description are
      replaced by Section~\ref{dpn:fp:sec:b77} and the README; its priority
      caveats are kept there.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Batch 100.}
Sources 100-82, 100-89 and 100-93 are Research Reports 82, 89 and 93 of the
session bundle of Reports 1--243, which arrived in commit \texttt{60f54ea06}
(5 October 2026); they formed one cluster of batch 100, whose placement
commit \texttt{36571ae0e} staged their files under the prefixes
\file{100-82-integral-}, \file{100-89-boundary-} and \file{100-93-theta-} and
retired the archives, which survive in the arrival commit.  The prefixes carry
the bundle report numbers, because batch 100's cluster-local numbers 01--03
are used by other clusters.  Their provenance manifests were verified at
placement (13/13, 19/19 and 39/39 files), as was source 100-93's diagnostics
checksum list (5/5, not shipped).  Source 100-93's README pins source 100-89's
archive by SHA-256 (\texttt{225b2070\ldots}); it matches.  The three are one
author series: source 100-89 calls source 100-82 ``the earlier A022629
report'', and source 100-93 calls source 100-89 its fixed-power companion.
None cites sources 40 to 77-51.  Their texts share $1.12\,\%$, $0.63\,\%$ and
$0.15\,\%$ of their word 8-grams with this report as it stood before the merge,
and $0.6$--$2.8\,\%$ with each other (status lines, preamble, standard
displays).  Sources 100-82 and 100-89 name no ProveIt commit; source 100-93 pins
\texttt{6591a56b3} \cite{proveit93}.  All three were delivered with
\file{verify_package.py}, \file{build_local.sh}, a README and a PDF, none of
which is shipped; no ``prepared for private review'' line, personal path or
third-party data occurs in them.

\emph{What each contributed.}  Source 100-89: Theorem~\ref{dpn:rb:thm:smooth}
and the closed forms and integer-power rule of
Section~\ref{dpn:rb:sec:boundary}; Theorem~\ref{dpn:rb:thm:allorders} and
Corollary~\ref{dpn:rb:cor:first}; Theorem~\ref{dpn:rb:thm:inverse} with the slope
bound of Proposition~\ref{dpn:rb:prop:slope}; the sub-block route and the
Sommerfeld and minimized-phase forms of Section~\ref{dpn:rb:sec:routes} (with
source 100-82); its table in Table~\ref{dpn:rb:tab:numerics}; three citations;
its non-claims.  Source 100-82: $P_9$, $P_{10}$, $\Delta_9$, $\Delta_{10}$
(Proposition~\ref{dpn:rb:prop:P910}, from its exact record); the case
\eqref{dpn:rb:eq:a1} with its $L^1$ and local-limit proofs; the resummed inverse
of Remark~\ref{dpn:rb:rem:resummed}; its table; its non-claims.  Source 100-93:
all of Section~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:theta} except the parts marked \wrt.

\emph{Where the batch-100 merge chose.}
\begin{itemize}
\item Placement: all three were added to this report; sources 100-82 and
      100-89 re-prove its main theorems, and source 100-93 answers parts of
      its projects~1, 4 and 6.  The report has no Parts, so, as in batches 75
      and 77, they became Sections~\ref{dpn:rb:sec:b100}
      and~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:theta}, after Section~\ref{dpn:fp:sec:b77} and before
      the appendices; every earlier number is unchanged.
\item Base of Section~\ref{dpn:rb:sec:b100}: source 100-89, the more general
      (every real $\alpha$, uniform on compacts); source 100-82 is its case
      $\alpha=1$, printed as a corollary, with its different proofs as second
      routes.  Source 100-93 has a regime of its own and a section of its own.
\item Duplicates: recorded in Table~\ref{dpn:rb:tab:dup}, not reprinted.
\item Renamed: source 100-89's Edgeworth order $M$ is written $J-1$, so that
      its theorems read with this report's $J$; every other renaming is in the
      fourth table of Section~\ref{dpn:sec:notation}.
\item Source 100-82's $P_9$, $P_{10}$, $\Delta_9$, $\Delta_{10}$ exist only in its
      JSON record; they are printed with that provenance and with the write's
      confirmation by source 75-06's program.
\item Source 100-89's two sketched steps are completed in the text
      (Lemma~\ref{dpn:rb:lem:holo}, Proposition~\ref{dpn:rb:prop:slope}); the
      sharp-scale form of its expansion (Remark~\ref{dpn:rb:rem:sharp}), the
      theta shift of the threshold (Remark~\ref{dpn:ct:rem:shift}) and the
      phase average \eqref{dpn:ct:eq:average} are the write's.  An independent
      check after the write found all five valid; one overstatement in
      Remark~\ref{dpn:ct:rem:shift} and one understated term in
      Remark~\ref{dpn:rb:rem:sharp} are corrected (dated note at the end of
      Section~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:questions}).
\item Source 100-93 is not presented as explaining this report's fixed-$\alpha$
      finite-size residuals: its hypotheses fail there, and its formula does
      not reproduce them (Section~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:relation}).  The intake's
      cell decomposition of those residuals is recorded as numerical evidence
      in Section~\ref{dpn:jh:sec:numerics}.
\item Its relation to the lattice-peak transfer theorem of the neighbouring
      report is stated in Section~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:transfer}; a reciprocal note
      there is proposed separately.
\item Abstracts, title pages, status paragraphs and package descriptions of
      the three sources are replaced by Section~\ref{dpn:rb:sec:b100},
      Section~\ref{dpn:ct:sec:theta} and the README; their priority caveats and
      non-claims are kept.  Source 100-89's Gikunda item is \cite{gikunda} with
      the DOI; its other items are \cite{GS}, \cite{BBBF}, \cite{AGW},
      \cite{Canton} and the OEIS entries already cited; source 100-82's are
      \cite{oeis1}, \cite{NR}, \cite{GS}; source 100-93's are \cite{oeisarray},
      source 100-89 and \cite{proveit93}.
\end{itemize}
